My question is: 119/M "The Austrian Federal Government is committed to introducing a financial transaction tax at European level. What concrete steps will be taken You will have your Since then, in order to push forward this common goal of the Federal Government?’
ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2382	Mr. Vice-Chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2397	Ladies and gentlemen! You are perfectly right, this is a joint project of the Federal Government. We must, on the one hand, strive with the partners within the European Union, but also internationally for us to take steps forward as regards the introduction of a financial transaction tax. What do I do personally? – On the one hand, I am closely connected with my French colleague Alain Juppé on this issue. You know that France now has the G8 presidency and wants to advance the project at international level. And we are closely aligned in this regard. Secondly, at European level, we must work with the partners who want to do so, especially those countries which are skeptical of the introduction of such a financial transaction tax. I will also take the opportunity to bring this issue back to the agenda as an EIA chairman in the framework of the European People's Party – this meeting before the next European Council is taking place very soon. I think that is very necessary.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2443	Minister! Should the introduction of this European, pan-European financial transaction tax not be feasible, how do you evaluate the alternative introduction of a financial transaction tax initially within the euro zone?
ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2458	Mr. Vice-Chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2474	I believe that we should first really try to achieve a breakthrough at international level within the G8 framework. This is desirable and also in the interest of Austrian companies and Austria and the European Union as a whole. If this does not work, the second level is that of the European Union. We should work on this. The third level would be that of the euro zone. But this is already a very limited variant and would certainly also bring competitive disadvantages to those who are in the euro zone. That is why this is not the first goal, but only the third one.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e6836	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen here in the house and in front of the screens! On today's motion for a law on electoral reform, I believe that it is very welcome that initiatives by the liberals, which have expressed criticism in particular of the election of letters, have been included in this proposal for reform, and do not want to recognise the government parties' efforts in this regard. In particular, the following points: Today, the swindling vote is abolished, i.e. the tactical election after the end of the election, which has been possible until now. It is very strange in itself that this was at all possible – we have criticized it from the beginning – but after all, one has followed our view here, and it is now being abolished. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] There will be an accurate examination of how many electoral cards have been issued and how many have been reached. There was also a very great possibility of manipulation because this question was open. - That too will be abolished. The time limits will be extended. Due to this change in the context of fraud voters, the deadlines will be moved forward so that foreign Austrians, for which the letter election is really important, will actually be allowed to vote. It will improve delivery – that was one of the biggest difficulties we know from the past. And finally, family members of the Habsburg family will be allowed the passive right to vote during the Federal President's election. A brief comment: The Freedom Party is the origin of democracy and the Republic. We are going back to the revolution of 1848 and now we have no historical explanation if we want to eliminate this curiosum, we are simply of the opinion that the earth has turned on and that this is therefore to be abolished. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] The choice of letters as such still needs to be presented here in order to clarify why the Freedom Party takes a different position from the government parties in this regard. The letter election was first and foremost introduced so that foreign Austrians have the opportunity to participate in the election, and as an exception to the domestic election – this is also formulated in the federal constitution. The choice of letters in the country was not quite understandable from the very beginning, because there is the possibility to take a vote and go to a different constituency, so to make the choice there. But there were also the flying constituencies for the disabled, the sick, for those who cannot get to the constituency. From the very beginning, decisive constitutional lawyers have always pointed out that all the principles of electoral rights, which are crucial to democracy, are not respected: the direct and personal right to vote – it is not voted as it is in the election hall; the secret right to vote – it is not clear whether anyone sees what vote is being cast here; the uninfluenced right to vote – even because of the same problem: Who is next to filling out the electoral card? Is this perhaps done by someone else?, and the general right to vote – ultimately, the post or the delivery decides whether I can exercise my right to vote, because if the election envelope is lost, I do not take part in the election and I am excluded from the election. It is a very strange thing that I can be taken by a third party the opportunity to take part in the election and then the electoral card is on my way to risk and I do not have the opportunity to see it. All these principles of election have not been complied with. It is a high culture in Austria, how elections take place. Every citizen knows that he has to show his ID in the constituency, that he can only go to the constituency cell on his own, that there must not even be a traffic jam, so that any irregularities do not happen, that you must not vote openly, close the envelope and throw it in. This is made quite consciously, because the choice is a very sensitive process. It used to be called: You can tell people what you want, you can influence them, they may also have another party book, but in the election cell, nobody looks at them, they vote as they really think! [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] That's exactly the case with the choice of letters and just not the case. Of course you can ask: Oh, my God, what are you upset about? – There are just too many specific cases that show that there are irregularities and that they are actually affected. Without now making any credit award or accusation an example that is quite amazing, from the last country election in Vienna, from my home district Simmering: There was the result in the normal constituency: 37 percent FPÖ and 47 percent SPÖ, so 10 percent difference. In the selection of letters alone – only in the selection of letters! – the FPÖ had 25 percent and the SPÖ 55 percent. [[Abg. Vilimsky: Look here!]] Now no one can tell me that we have a different section of voters here. If the Greens or the ÖVP have significantly higher shares in the letter voters, it has always been said that these are the more educated layers, more flexible persons or anything else. But the SPÖ and the FPÖ certainly have a very similar audience with the voters in Simmering, and therefore this result is quite amazing. I do not claim that election fraud has taken place here, but there is simply the possibility of influence, there is the possibility of exerting pressure. [[Congratulation with the FPÖ and acclaim of the Abg. Windwood.]] This is very subtle: You can suggest to a person: Take part in the election of letters, it's not difficult at all, I'll help you apply for the electoral card! And then, of course, the party that has a high degree of organisation knows when the election card arrives, and then someone can point it out again: Come on, I'll help you! At least then – so far it was even easier at all – to pick it up. The person must first resist the choice of letter. Someone has to trust it first! We know that there are enough people in Vienna – in small communities it is certainly the same – who somehow have respect when someone comes, an authority, and so suggests. The person must be so strong, so strong, or so courageous to resist. Or this person then gives up their choice of letter – that's the name on it. How does the simple citizen know that what he has chosen cannot be understood? He doesn't know exactly how the process is going. We, who are sitting here or who are also in the electoral commissions, know how this is done or how it should be done. The simple citizen doesn't know that, he's now giving up this election card with his name. And that there is actually no influence, no one can assume. And that's exactly the point. If you take the right to vote as seriously as we should take it seriously, you still have to treat the choice of letter very sensitively. We believe that within the Austrian state, we do not need a choice of letters in the national elections, because there is the possibility of going to another constituency with a vote card, there is the possibility of the flying election commission – and apart from that, one or two weeks before that, for people who actually want to travel, so that they have the opportunity to carry out their choice properly. That would actually make sense. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] There will now be the argument that there is a choice of letters in fifty states on earth. I can only say: There are also nuclear energy and nuclear power plants in dozens of countries on earth. I hope that this is not really the absolute argument, but we have to deal with how we regulate this domestically, whether this is a problem in the country and how we evaluate it based on our federal constitution, our fundamental rights. So don't come up with the argument that there's something else. Then the argument will come, and we have thus increased voting participation. – First of all, this is simple and simply not true: For example, voting participation has increased by 0.3 percent in the National Council election, i.e. to an undetectable extent at all, or has even fallen. [[Abg. Dr. Bartenstein:... sunk!]] But in any case, an increase is in no way detectable. And the third argument is to simplify it with that. – Now you have to ask yourself: Is it really the point of saying that you have to simplify the right to vote, we sacrifice the basic values and the basic ideas of the right to vote for it to become more comfortable? [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] Does this not also mean that the right to vote loses value to the citizen if I make it easier for him? This does not improve political starry. If one makes it clear to the citizen what an important process is, how sensitive it is to choose, and that this ultimately makes the decision on who then rules in that state, then it is much more important than to say: Out of convenience we do something here and assume in purchase that the basic ideas of the right to vote are not accepted! It was also very interesting at the expert hearing in the Constitutional Committee that, for example, the expert Professor Mayer, nominated by SPÖ, said quite clearly here: It may not be unconstitutional, but it is extremely difficult to maintain the right to vote in this form. That's why my appeal is: It is crucial for the cohesion of this society that election results are accepted that the population assumes that election results have been properly achieved. If one day it starts to crumble and one has the impression that there is not really everything right here when sections of the population have the impression that pressure has been exerted on me, and I can no longer simply go to the election cell like this, because I am expected to do the post-election somewhere, so if there are real difficulties here and the population no longer has the impression that the election has been carried out freely and properly, then this state also has a problem. I therefore call on you, in this sense, to abolish the choice of letters in the country and to proceed in accordance with the ideas set out by us.

This is in itself the expression of the fact that it is after 90 years of time that we are quite in line with these current requirements here. Nor should one imagine the hundreds of family members of the Habsburgs in such a way that they only run around with wigs or with a determined look at counter-reformation, but that there is quite a differentiated picture in this family, with members who may even be on the left, and those who have very reactionary ideas. Since probably not only are present in the House of Habsburg today, but probably in addition to seeing Habsburg during this debate, I would like to share a few thoughts with the Habsburg Family Council so that they do not believe that we are confusing normality in a normal electoral process in the context of history or even the explanation of history. It is likely that the Habsburg family, which is present here today, will vote in favour of it anyway, but will only strengthen its position in the Habsburg family if these discussions begin. This includes, among other things, the asset issue. It is not by chance stated in the Habsburg Act under § 7 that the reinertragnis is transferred to the property of the population, the Republic of Austria, among other things, to those who are harmed by the World War in their health or who are deprived of their feeder. This is very important because for decades there has always been this debate: What about the wealth, and did not even some private property have been unjustly taken away from the Habsburgs? Now it is a matter of finding historically that the Habsburgs were not entirely innocent at the outbreak of the First World War and that it was only right and cheap that they began to transfer this property to the property of the population as a kind of compensation for what they had to suffer and endure at the time. That is why we now have an indication in the legal regulation that the provisions of the Habsburg Act, which in particular concern the assets, are not affected. We also have an indication that this was also set out in the State Treaty. This means that there are several points to this: It's not touched! One was added to the comments made by the expert Professor Mayer in the Constitutional Committee: The State must be able to ensure compliance with the principles, namely secret, direct, personal, thus unimpeded by external impressions, and must not overturn this duty on the shoulders of the election subject. If so the father-in-law, who looks over the shoulder with the clan to the crusader, says: If you do the right thing in the right place, he would have the duty to chase the others away. No, it's not like that! The State has to protect the "electional Law" institution and ensure the principles "secret", "immediate", "personal" by the right to vote. Therefore, we will not abandon the current form of the right of choice of letter, although today's improvement in the removal of the tactical election is valuable. Mr Stadler has done the right thing about the story with the prisoners' election. There's nothing to add to that. But still: We are not happy with today's process, regardless of the fact that two improvement points are included in the system. But today's progress will not stop tomorrow's progress. - Thank you.

That one can no longer speak of the exercise of the secret right to vote is obvious. [[Abg. May. Gaßner: Where was that?]] In Upper Austria. [[Abg. Rädler: Who exactly was that?]] I don't know now, but I can deliver to you. There is also the long-known case of a mayor in Burgenland, who has filled out electoral cards in a larger style. There is also the presumption with regard to the Vienna election that there were large-scale tickets for whole groups in the style of individuals. You can also assume or suspect that these electoral cards were then filled in in one and never landed with the voters for whom they were ordered. And there are also the large groups of persons who live in nursing homes affected by dementia and who are not able to get out of these institutions on the electoral day and are therefore ordered to vote for them without knowing that their families were ever asked. There you can very well discuss whether these people are still in a position to take this concrete will and then make a specific choice. Against this background, it was particularly important to us – and this was also stated in requests – that all these possibilities should be excluded, not because we do not believe in the power of the electorate, but because we have been confirmed what was feared, namely that not all institutions and not all political actors feel committed to democratic principles and that there has been no abuse at all. Against this background, we are pleased, of course, that everything has now been done, although we must also say that there was still much room for abuse in the first part of the request submitted by SPÖ, ÖVP and BZÖ. We are also very pleased that we have had the opportunity to submit our proposals. Now it is clearly defined: Application of the electoral card only more either verbally or in writing. What we still lack is the clear definition that the application of the electoral card must take place in front of the office and must not take place in the marketplace, where perhaps the mayor comes by and says: Hey, don't you need a election card, I'll organize it? It is important that identity certificates are required and that the communities are also authorised to review them. That was not possible until now. It is also important that the transmission of the electoral cards is carried out by registered letter and that the transmission of the electoral cards to the home residents is designed in such a way that they must confirm that they have taken over the card, and that in the case of the messengers who have now been introduced and who are to transfer the electoral cards, it is clear that not the same messengers that bring the card wait until the voter has filled this card in to take it back, but that the two different persons must be. It has also been mentioned that there is a joint motion for a resolution to be examined: What about the criminal provisions, how are they currently being applied, are they sufficient or does it need legislative measures and legal acts? After the hearing and even after the talks with us, we managed to turn this automatic and that it is not now automatically all prisoners, but only those with whom the judge has it. Let us take a look at the jurisdiction of the European Court of Human Rights. I assume that as a minister of this Republic you feel committed to these human rights, the EMRK. The standards are relatively clear: The right to vote must not be removed by law in general. Secondly, It must be done by a judge's decision, and thirdly: It can only be done if the crime committed is related to the threat of democracy and the rule of law. Now you make a law where you obviously want to give the impression, even if someone commits a serious crime, he may be deprived of the right to vote by making it unclear. They say: The right to vote may be withdrawn if it is required in the light of the circumstances of the individual case. I assume that the European courts will interpret this empty formula – I have to put it this way – that this is only possible in a limited segment, namely in the event of a threat to democracy and the rule of law, in the light of the EGMR Judicature, and that, of course, the right to vote will always be withdrawn in the future only if certain political offences exist. So it's not true if you make yourself there and claim relatively sharp if someone has committed a serious crime, then he won't be allowed to vote in the future. [[Abg. Grosz: Let us make an actual correction that is not full moon!]] This issue was discussed throughout Europe, and there was also a committee of inquiry in Austria. Two themes must be distinguished in this area. The first concerns opinion research prior to the elections, the second concerns the publication of partial results before the end of the election. We do not think that legal measures and prohibitions regarding the publication of election forecasts are useful. I believe that it is rather necessary to pay attention to compliance with certain rules of the game, such as the directive published by the ESOMAR. Certain minimum requirements need to be met, especially in the field of opinion research, especially in the publication of survey results, about what basic information there was for questioning, how the range of variations was, how the exact wording of the question was or how the percentage of respondents was. A clear distinction must also be made between the results of the survey and the comments and interpretation of the same. Ladies and gentlemen! Legal bans not only present legal problems, for example, when we consider Article 10 of the ECHR on freedom of expression, but also the question of how we intend to enforce such bans. Especially in times of the Internet, a circumvention is easily possible. The Austrian rule of law does not have access to websites on which survey results are published, running on a non-Austrian domain. Second topic, publication of partial results prior to the election: I do not believe that the legal ban on publication is also a target for this subject. As has just been said, the Internet and the circumvention possibilities play a major role here, of course. I believe that in this case it is worth considering whether to feed these partial results into a specific database and provide them only to a limited number of participants, or whether to arrange a uniform conclusion of the electoral venues throughout the country. This request should therefore be rejected by the BZÖ. What I would like to discuss in this context, however, is § 7 of the Volksbehrengesetz, which Mr Singer has already mentioned. § 7 refers to the registration times for popular desires. The registration shall be kept open on working days from 8 am to 4 pm, on two working days in addition to 8 pm, on Saturdays and Sundays at least from 8 am to 12 pm. In municipalities with less than 2,500 inhabitants on Saturdays and Sundays, the registration time can be reduced to two consecutive hours. It is now the question of how many people really use this registration time on a Sunday. The communities – especially the smaller ones – complain, and the complaints become more. I think that this is also really worth a discussion, and I hope that this debate has been a start to it today. - Thank you.

Another small observation that I was able to make even in previous elections as mayor, namely that, because of this right to vote, we could greatly reduce the so-called special electoral authority – the flying electoral authority – in some elections or even not to take advantage of it at all. This is also a relief for us and reduces the number of electoral authorities in a municipality. Ladies and gentlemen, summarized: I think that this electoral change law will bring greater clarity and security to the individual people and will be more practical for the citizen as well. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e10554	Mr President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors! Appreciate Members. The Court of Auditors has examined the implementation of the emergency police call in Austria. It should be noted that there has actually been the discussion and the plan for the introduction of a uniform police emergency system throughout Austria for ten years. There are currently 105 emergency service stations, 13 urban areas, 83 in the districts and 9 national authorities. The objective is quite clear, namely to employ and implement 9 national governments in a uniform manner. This report shows relatively clearly – and it is indeed a very productive report, Mr President – that there is the possibility of optimising and improving everywhere in the police emergency system. This report gives a great insight into the system and the digital network development and clearly shows weaknesses. He also shows the abuse when using the emergency call number, which is a large share, because only 20 to 25 percent are actually emergency calls. The fact is that there are large differences between countries. Vienna and Vorarlberg have implemented the system neatly and optimally. It is currently in implementation in Salzburg and Tyrol. That was what Mrs Fekter told us in committee at the time. In Upper Austria, the least has happened. There are currently negotiations with the country, so that the Federation, which provides the hardware, is there. The criticisms were that personnel resources were in no relation to the burden of the emergency call, that there is no knowledge of key figures of police deployments – that should actually not happen in the present time – and that there is no recording of waiting times. It takes up to 20 seconds for the caller to make contact with the emergency call system. The design of the workplace is also partly inadequate or only slightly in line with the requirements. I've already talked about heavy abuse. It is just done via the mobile phones, from which you can trigger the emergency call even without SIM card. Germany currently has the system that can only be done with SIM card, and the Court of Auditors has recommended that this system be introduced from Germany. However, the then Federal Minister has said that she also wants this emergency call system to be accessible to all. We shall still see what the current Minister of the Interior will then implement in practice. There are also different figures from police and Telekom. The recommendations were made very clearly by the Court of Auditors, and their implementation is actively being carried out: a central guide in each country, which records all emergency calls; Clarification of the deviation of key figures, but of course also the preparation of a concept in order to obtain meaningful key figures. In future, it is also important to reduce, reduce and optimise the waiting times for emergency calls and the use of human resources. It is also important for the Court of Auditors to call for security forces to remain secure. Overall, this Court of Auditors report is a comprehensive and productive contribution – I have already said this in the first place – in order to advance the comprehensive development of an operational management system in Austria. It was also assured that the Court’s recommendations will be implemented. I hope that this will be accelerated in the near future, that there will be a contact point per state, an emergency call number. The Minister of the Interior has also reported that the new emergency call number 112, which has a European character, is to be applied more strongly, because this offers more security to all people and serves the population. The central control of the forces is also important, because callers often do not know whether the police need it, the rescue or the fire brigade, and that is to be controlled from this control system. Total: Thank you to the Court of Auditors and also to the countries that have already implemented the proposals. The summaries are supposed to take an example of the punctuality, then in the near future we will get an emergency call system in Austria, which works, the resources are used properly and helps people in distress. Thank you.

Why? – To reduce taxes alone leads to less tax revenue and thus to more debt and higher indebtedness. [[Abg. Booker: This is a fool! That's an absolute nonsense!]] This is a way that we Social Democrats do not want to go. Mrs Fekter, it is totally nice that you appreciate me so young, but Mr Matznetter and I were praised in 2002 on the same day. That's why I know how it was with the cold-down. The phenomenon is relatively simple. Köst is a business-dependent tax. That is, if the economy is high, profits are high and the tax yield is high, and if the economy is poor, the profits are low and the tax almost doesn't bring any money at all. If we look at what Köst brought in 2008/2009, this was not a bagatell tax yet, but very, very little. If you lower the tax rate in an economicly bad time and compare it with the revenue in the high economy, they are higher – nonet!. Of course, a reduction in taxes means less taxes and no more at all if the economic factor is properly calculated. The phenomenon is called economics, and that is easy to explain. If we say yes – and I hope we all agree – we have to and want to reduce the tax burden on earnings/work income, then on the other hand there are only two variants. One thing is to make more debts. The other thing is that those who do not make a fair contribution today or who make a much smaller contribution than benefit income today also make a fair contribution. [[Abg. Head: And spend less!]] I believe that if we look at how the income distribution is and how the tax burden is distributed, we must all be of the opinion that labour certainly makes a too high contribution and that capital and assets really have a lot of scope, especially in the international comparison. If we only implement the tax on property, which is taken on average internationally, we would have more tax revenue by €5 billion. [[Abg. Head: That is not our goal! 5 billion € more revenue cannot be the goal!]] If we just cut it, we would have €5 billion more. [[Abg. Bucher: Then the capital is gone!]] If we only had the cut, then we could also reduce taxes and taxes on work by €5 billion. We would have the same thing under the rule, but we would have a much more just distribution than we have today. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and Greens.]] I think that is an essential question. This House, the legislator, has the Austrian Members, so it must be said, equipped with a corruption privilege. When this House implemented the UN Convention against Corruption in 2008, a comprehensive crime for corruption of Members should have been adopted. I can still remember the meeting. The aim has been to keep this criminal offence as narrow as possible. At that time it was even explained that it did not matter if you did not implement the UN Convention, because that was not the case. One thing you really have to say: There is a group of delegates here, a party for which victims are not victims and the dead are not dead, who only want to see a group of victims! [[Abg. May. Kogler: What?- Oh, my God!]] All the victims who do not fit into their scheme did not exist, so to speak. [[Call the ÖVP:Genau!]] In any case, as the colleague said earlier so beautifully, one can “dispute”, one can look at it “unemotionally”, perhaps again, but one cannot say what it is, one cannot say it is a terrible crime, and one cannot say that we have been wrong too! We have honored terrible criminals, genocides, mass murderers, and we are so far today that we sit down for me “unmotional” and say that this honor was a mistake and we want to reverse it. What is there to discuss? What's wrong with it? – Mr Oellinger, your people are voting in Vienna [[Intrusion of the Abg. Oils.]] Municipal council, of course, against removing the Stalin memorial plaque on Schönbrunner Straße. [[Uh-Rufe at FPÖ, BZÖ and ÖVP.]] Yes, Stalin is a “super man ”! – Every time the Greens voted against it, there was a request to remove this real scandal poster! [[Call the SPÖ: State contract!]] There were such arguments as, for example, well, that's secured in the State Treaty. – Do you know who in the early 1990s asked to remove this plaque? – Ambassador to the Russian Federation! The Russian Federation, which, if there is any such one, is the successor of the Soviet Union, has asked to remove this plaque! In the Vienna municipal council this is regularly rejected, with reference to the state treaty, of course with the votes of the Greens. This has to be clarified in this House once! There is one group that is not prepared to distance itself from atrocities, violence, mass murders and to condemn them clearly – and that is the Green Group! Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e18543	Mr Dr. Walser spoke on an actual correction. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e18582	Mr President. Parliamentary Scheibner claimed that I had relativized and denied the crimes of the Tito parties. – That’s wrong! It is true, rather, that I have pointed out that these crimes are undisputed, that they will not be questioned at all in the sense of historian, that the contribution of the Tito Army to the liberation of Austria must not be left to the table, because this contribution was essential to the fact that thousands of Carinthian Slovenians survived this war. [[Abg. Dr. Strutz: It's not in the record!]] For the further context is important...
ParlaMint-AT_2011-06-16-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00110_d7e18611	Thank you for the correction and comparison. [[I would like to congratulate the Greens for the Abg, which leaves the speaker's desk. Dr. Walser.]] There are no more words available to me. This concludes the first reading on the application concerning a federal law amending the Federal Law on the Creation of Honorary Merits for the Republic of Austria.
